- 2. Qxd2
- 2. Ke2
- 2. Kf1

And the queen is on d2.

This seems very strong.

1... c6e5.
Now White must deal with the pressure on d3 and f3.
If White plays 2. d2xe4, then ...d5xe4.
Now Black has a knight on e5 and a pawn on e4.
This is also very strong.

A) 1... c6e5 (keeping the knight on e4)
B) 1... e4d2+ (trading knights)

In (A), White can trade his d2-knight for the e4-knight.
In (B), Black forces the trade.

Professional players usually prefer to keep their most active pieces.
The knight on e4 is more active than the knight on d2.
So ...c6e5 is probably the better positional move.
It maintains the pressure and forces White to make a decision.

It's a strong, centralizing move that maintains the pieces and increases pressure.
